2026-10-10T02:20:47.2777276Z shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}
2026-10-10T02:20:47.2777631Z env:
2026-10-10T02:20:47.2777992Z   UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/uv-python-dir
2026-10-10T02:20:47.2778428Z   UV_CACHE_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/setup-uv-cache
2026-10-10T02:20:47.2786993Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [require-build] [cluster:aspire2ap] [nodes:2] Use twoshot fused all-reduce for single-decode verify batches above 128 tokens (P3/D1 c72)

Why: in the bounded decode Nsight capture (f84ea21, ~63 running requests,
~252 verify tokens per step) the FlashInfer TRT-LLM all-reduce + residual
+ RMSNorm fusion took 5.6 ms per step on rank 0: 187 calls of
allreduce_fusion_kernel_oneshot_lamport at ~30 us for ~2 MB of BF16. It is
the third largest decode cost after MoE weight streaming and FP8
attention. FlashInfer 0.6.15 chooses the strategy with B200-tuned
heuristics (trtllm_ar.py _use_oneshot_heuristics: TP4 oneshot while
token_num*hidden*2*world*2 <= 64 MiB, i.e. up to 1024 tokens). The fixed
SGLang call always passes use_oneshot=None. Oneshot makes every rank read
all peers' full buffers (3x the payload at TP4); twoshot reduce-scatters
and all-gathers (~1.5x), which should suit H100 NVLink at ~2 MB.

Source change (new patch decode-allreduce-twoshot.patch, applied after the
four existing ones; the XQA patch was reverted in b9ac4eb):
flashinfer_comm_fusion.flashinfer_allreduce_residual_rmsnorm reads the
optional SGLANG_FLASHINFER_AR_ONESHOT_MAX_TOKENS once at import. When it is
set and the caller passes no strategy, calls above max(threshold,
world_size) tokens use twoshot. Only the fusion call's use_oneshot changes:
workspace initialization still receives None, so no re-initialization
(collective) can occur during graph capture, and FlashInfer documents that
the TRT-LLM workspace supports both strategies. Unset keeps today's
behavior. Both strategies sum identical inputs with the same
accumulation precision; only reduction order may differ.

Runtime change: configs/aspire-serving.json decode_allreduce_oneshot_max_tokens
128 (SGLang's own min-latency oneshot comment uses 128) exported to the
decode worker only. Serving is otherwise ed8c534: P3/D1, decode pool
393216, c72, FA3, page size 1, Triton decode MoE, same EAGLE3 settings.
Prefill chunks (8192 > max_token_num 2048) never take this fused path.

Expected: up to ~2-3 ms less per verify step at ~60 running requests
(~5-8% ITL), more decode headroom for P3/D1. Judge against ed8c534 by
score, ITL vs running, accept length and greedy probes; not a matched pair.

Budget: see the myprojects check in the session log before this push;
this job reserves at most 1024 SU.

Validation: full CPU pytest including the actual five-patch chain on the
fixed source mirror (asserting the override precedes the fusion kwargs),
the decode argv fixture with the new environment, Ruff and bash -n.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

heuristics (trtllm_ar.py _use_oneshot_heuristics: TP4 oneshot while
token_num*hidden*2*world*2 <= 64 MiB, i.e. up to 1024 tokens). The fixed
SGLang call always passes use_oneshot=None. Oneshot makes every rank read
all peers'' full buffers (3x the payload at TP4); twoshot reduce-scatters
and all-gathers (~1.5x), which should suit H100 NVLink at ~2 MB.

Why: in the bounded decode Nsight capture (f84ea21, ~63 running requests,
~252 verify tokens per step) the FlashInfer TRT-LLM all-reduce + residual
+ RMSNorm fusion took 5.6 ms per step on rank 0: 187 calls of
allreduce_fusion_kernel_oneshot_lamport at ~30 us for ~2 MB of BF16. It is
the third largest decode cost after MoE weight streaming and FP8
attention. FlashInfer 0.6.15 chooses the strategy with B200-tuned
heuristics (trtllm_ar.py _use_oneshot_heuristics: TP4 oneshot while
token_num*hidden*2*world*2 <= 64 MiB, i.e. up to 1024 tokens). The fixed
SGLang call always passes use_oneshot=None. Oneshot makes every rank read
all peers'' full buffers (3x the payload at TP4); twoshot reduce-scatters
and all-gathers (~1.5x), which should suit H100 NVLink at ~2 MB.
